> AGENT

The "14" is not actually today's orders — that's a labeling bug.

**What's happening** (`src/app/farmer/dashboard/page.tsx:372`):

```js
const pendingCount = pendingOrders.filter((o) => o.status === 'pending').length
```

The card labeled **"Today's orders"** (line 487) actually renders `pendingCount` (line 486). `pendingCount` is the count of **all orders still in `pending` status** — every order awaiting your approval, regardless of the date it was placed. The query that feeds it (line 193) has no date filter at all; it pulls all pending/approved/unacknowledged-cancelled orders.

So **14 = you have 14 orders sitting in "pending" (awaiting approval)**, accumulated over time — not 14 orders placed today.

There's also an inconsistency: the card links to `/farmer/dashboard/orders?time=today` (line 481), so tapping it filters the orders list to *today*, which will likely show a different (smaller) number than the 14 on the tile.

**Two ways to fix it**, depending on what you actually want the tile to mean:

1. **Make the number match the label** — count orders created today (to match the `?time=today` link).
2. **Make the label match the number** — rename the tile to "Pending orders" / "Orders to approve" since it's really a pending-approval count.

Which behavior do you want? My recommendation is option 1 (count actual today's orders) since the tile already deep-links to the today filter, so the number and the destination would finally agree.

Type-check passes. Done.

## What I changed

In `src/app/farmer/dashboard/page.tsx`, the **"Today's orders"** tile now shows orders actually placed today instead of the all-time pending count:

1. **Added a `todayCount` state** and a dedicated count query that pulls every order for the farmer with `created_at >= local midnight` — **any status** — so it matches exactly what the tile's `?time=today` link shows when tapped.
2. **Tile now renders `todayCount`** (number + orange highlight) instead of `pendingCount`.
3. **Realtime INSERT** bumps `todayCount` so a new order placed today updates the tile live.

`pendingCount` is untouched — it still drives the "X orders need your response" urgency banner, which is genuinely about pending approvals.

Now the tile and its destination agree: if it says 3, tapping it shows those same 3 orders from today. The "14" you saw was 14 orders stuck in pending across all dates, which now correctly stays in the banner rather than masquerading as today's count.

To see it: `npm run dev` → farmer dashboard. Want me to commit this?

```
8	  vegetables: [

10	    'gourd', 'onion', 'potato', 'garlic', 'carrot', 'radish', 'cucumber',
11	    'okra', 'ladyfinger', 'drumstick', 'ridge gourd', 'bottle gourd', 'chilli',

15	    'mango', 'banana', 'papaya', 'guava', 'pomegranate', 'orange', 'lime',
16	    'lemon', 'coconut', 'tamarind', 'jackfruit', 'sapota', 'fig', 'grapes',
17	    'watermelon', 'muskmelon', 'pineapple', 'custard apple',
18	  ],
19	  grains: [
20	    'rice', 'wheat', 'jowar', 'bajra', 'ragi', 'maize', 'corn', 'dal',
21	    'lentil', 'chickpea', 'groundnut', 'sesame', 'turmeric', 'ginger',

23	  ],

30	export async function GET(request: NextRequest) {
31	  const { searchParams } = new URL(request.url)
32	  const q = searchParams.get('q') ?? ''
33	  const method = searchParams.get('method') ?? ''
34	  const category = searchParams.get('category') ?? ''
35	
36	  const { data: produce } = await supabase
37	    .from('produce_listings')
38	    .select('*')
39	    .eq('status', 'available')
40	    .order('created_at', { ascending: false })
41	
42	  let filtered = produce ?? []
43	
44	  if (q) {
45	    const query = q.toLowerCase()
46	    filtered = filtered.filter(
47	      (p) =>
48	        p.name?.toLowerCase().includes(query) ||
49	        p.variety?.toLowerCase().includes(query) ||
50	        p.description?.toLowerCase().includes(query)
51	    )
52	  }
53	
54	  if (method && method !== 'all') {
55	    filtered = filtered.filter((p) =>
56	      p.method?.toLowerCase().includes(method.toLowerCase())
57	    )
58	  }
59	
60	  if (category && category !== 'all') {
61	    const keywords = CATEGORY_KEYWORDS[category] ?? []
62	    // Prefer the farmer's explicit category (#9); only fall back to guessing from
63	    // the crop name for older listings that don't have a category set yet.
64	    filtered = filtered.filter((p) =>
65	      p.category
66	        ? p.category === category
67	        : keywords.some((kw) => p.name?.toLowerCase().includes(kw))
68	    )
69	  }
70
```

> AGENT

Type-check passes. Done — **Spices** and **Other** are now added everywhere produce categories are enumerated:

1. **Farmer add/edit produce form** (`farmer/dashboard/page.tsx`) — two new `<option>`s, bilingual labels (Spices = మసాలాలు, Other = ఇతర).
2. **Consumer filter chips** (`consumer/page.tsx`) — two new filter chips with EN/TE labels.
3. **Produce detail page** (`consumer/produce/[id]/page.tsx`) — `CATEGORY_LABEL` map so the category badge renders for these.
4. **Search/filter API** (`api/produce/search/route.ts`) — a `spices` keyword list (turmeric, ginger, chilli, pepper, cardamom, etc.) so older listings without an explicit category still match by crop name. `other` is intentionally a catch-all with no keywords — it only matches listings a farmer explicitly tags as "Other".

I now have a complete picture. Before I change anything, here's my understanding:

## What I understand

You have **two different progress trackers** for the same order, and you want the **farmer's to match the consumer's**.

**Consumer order details (Image #1)** — progress shows:
- Order placed → Payment received → Confirmed by farmer → **Shipped** → **Delivered**

**Farmer order details (Image #2)** — progress shows:
- Order placed → Approved by you → **Rider assigned** → **Picked up by rider** → **Out for delivery** → Delivered

The farmer side still has the old **rider/delivery-boy flow** (the three steps: *Rider assigned, Picked up by rider, Out for delivery*). The consumer side was already switched to the simple **Shipped → Delivered** flow.

This matches your earlier decision (in my notes): *"farmer taps Shipped, buyer taps Delivered for every order type; the rider/handover routes are dead (return 410)."* So the farmer screen is showing a tracker that no longer reflects how orders actually move.

**What you want me to do:**
- On the farmer order-details page, **delete the rider branch** (Rider assigned / Picked up by rider / Out for delivery).
- Make the farmer's progress steps **mirror the consumer's**: Order placed → (Payment received, if paid online) → Confirmed/Approved → Shipped → Delivered — so both stay in sync.

```
56	  'id, farmer_id, order_code, produce_name, quantity, unit, total_price, buyer_name, buyer_phone, pickup_location, status, payment_method, payment_status, utr_number, decline_reason, refund_status, refund_amount, refunded_at, created_at, confirmed_at, paid_at, delivery_type, delivery_status, delivery_boy_id, delivery_address, delivery_landmark, delivery_pincode, delivery_alt_phone, assigned_at, picked_up_at, out_for_delivery_at, delivered_at, collected_at, shipped_at, received_at, fulfillment_date, acknowledged_at'

58	export default function FarmerOrderDetailPage() {
59	  const params = useParams<{ id: string }>()
60	  const id = typeof params?.id === 'string' ? params.id : ''
61	  const router = useRouter()
62	  const { tx, L } = useLang()
63	  const [order, setOrder] = useState<Order | null>(null)
64	  const [rider, setRider] = useState<{ name: string | null; phone: string } | null>(null)
65	  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(true)
66	  const [notFound, setNotFound] = useState(false)
67	
68	  useEffect(() => {
69	    if (!id) return
70	    const farmerId = localStorage.getItem('yff_farmer_id')
71	    if (!farmerId) { router.replace('/farmer/login'); return }
72	
73	    let cancelled = false
74	    setLoading(true)
75	    // Scope the read to this farmer's own orders so the detail page can never
76	    // surface another farmer's order, even via a guessed id.
77	    supabase
78	      .from('orders')
79	      .select(ORDER_COLUMNS)
80	      .eq('id', id)
81	      .eq('farmer_id', farmerId)
82	      .maybeSingle()
83	      .then(async ({ data }) => {
84	        if (cancelled) return
85	        if (!data) { setNotFound(true); setLoading(false); return }
86	        // Best-effort reschedule reason: the column may not exist until its
87	        // migration is applied, so fetch it separately and ignore failure.
88	        const { data: rs } = await supabase
89	          .from('orders')
90	          .select('reschedule_reason')
91	          .eq('id', id)
92	          .maybeSingle()
93	        if (cancelled) return
94	        setOrder({ ...(data as Order), reschedule_reason: (rs as { reschedule_reason?: string | null } | null)?.reschedule_reason ?? null })
95	        setLoading(false)
96	      })
97	    return () => { cancelled = true }
98	  }, [id, router])
99
```

> AGENT

Done. The farmer order-details progress now mirrors the consumer's exactly.

## What I changed

**File:** `src/app/farmer/dashboard/orders/[id]/page.tsx`

- **Deleted the rider branch** — *Rider assigned*, *Picked up by rider*, *Out for delivery* are gone, along with the per-type forks (*Received by buyer* / *Picked up by buyer*).
- **The farmer now sees the same 5-step flow as the consumer:**
  - Order placed
  - Payment received *(only for online-paid orders — UPI/Razorpay, just like the consumer)*
  - Approved by you *(you chose to keep the farmer's own wording rather than "Confirmed by farmer")*
  - Shipped — green with its date once you tap Shipped
  - Delivered — green with its date once the buyer confirms (`received_at`/`collected_at`)
- Added a local `isOnlinePayment()` helper (same logic the consumer uses) so the payment step appears under identical conditions.

This satisfies the Trello card too: when Shipped is pressed, that step goes green and stamps its date; Delivered does the same when the buyer confirms — and both screens stay in sync.

Type-check passes.

**One thing I left alone:** there's a small *rider contact card* (lines ~298–303) that only renders if an order actually has an assigned `delivery_boy_id`. Since the rider flow is dead, new orders never set that, so it won't appear — but old orders that once had a rider would still show it. I left it as-is since it's harmless and removing it is a separate cleanup. Want me to strip that out too, or commit what's here?

> AGENT

Root cause confirmed. The `farm-images` **storage bucket works fine** (full public upload/read policies), but the **`public.media` table has RLS enabled with only a SELECT policy — no INSERT or DELETE policy**. So:

- The photo file uploads to storage successfully, but
- the `media` row insert (`page.tsx:3102`) is **silently rejected by RLS** (the code never checks that insert's error), so no row is created → nothing shows in the dashboard or on the profile.

Let me check how `produce_listings` policies are written so I add a consistent one for `media`.

> AGENT

Type-check passes. Done.

## Root cause

"Add Farm Photo" uploaded the image file to storage fine, but the **`public.media` table had RLS enabled with only a SELECT policy** — no INSERT policy. The farmer dashboard uses the anon Supabase client (localStorage auth), so its insert into `media` was **silently rejected by RLS**. No `media` row was ever created, so the photo showed nowhere — not in the dashboard, not on the public profile. The code also ignored that insert's error, which is why it looked like nothing happened.

## The fix (two parts)

1. **Database (the actual fix — already applied to live Supabase):** Added the missing write policies to `public.media`, mirroring the permissive pattern already on `produce_listings`:
   - `INSERT` (anon)
   - `UPDATE`
   - `DELETE` (so the photo's "×" delete button works too — it had the same gap)
   
   Saved as `scripts/media-anon-write-policies.sql` for the repo.

2. **Code (`farmer/dashboard/page.tsx`):** The upload handler now checks the insert error and shows "Could not save photo: …" instead of silently swallowing it, so any future failure is visible.

**This works right now** — the RLS fix is live on the database, so adding a farm photo will persist and appear in both the dashboard and the profile immediately, no deploy needed. The code change (the error message) takes effect on your next deploy but isn't required for the feature to work.

One note: there was already **1 existing `media` photo row** in the DB. If that one isn't showing either, it's a separate display issue on the profile side — want me to check how the farmer profile renders the gallery to confirm it picks these up? And want me to commit these changes?

```
858	  const handlePickFile = async (

860	    setFile: (f: File | null) => void,

862	    currentPreview: string,
863	  ) => {
864	    const file = e.target.files?.[0]
865	    if (!file) return
866	    if (!file.type.startsWith('image/')) { setError(tx.pickImageFile); return }
867	    if (file.size > 8 * 1024 * 1024) { setError(tx.imageTooLarge); return }
868	    setError('')
869	    if (currentPreview) URL.revokeObjectURL(currentPreview)
870	    const compressed = await compressImage(file)
871	    setFile(compressed)
872	    setPreview(URL.createObjectURL(compressed))
873	  }
874	
875	  const uploadProfileImage = async (file: File, pathSuffix: string): Promise<{ url: string | null; err: string | null }> => {
876	    const ext = file.name.split('.').pop()?.toLowerCase() || 'jpg'
877	    // Include timestamp in path so each upload gets a unique URL, busting browser cache
878	    const path = `${farmer.id}/${pathSuffix}-${Date.now()}.${ext}`
```

## What was missing & what I added

The `water_source` column exists in the database and is already **displayed** on the public farmer profile (StoryTab → "Water source"), but the **Farmer Edit Profile modal had no input for it** — so farmers could never set it, and it always showed "—" on their profile.

**Changes in `src/app/farmer/dashboard/page.tsx`:**
1. Added `water_source` to the `Farmer` type.
2. Added a `waterSource` state in `ProfileEditModal`, initialized from `farmer.water_source`.
3. Added it to the save payload (`water_source: waterSource.trim() || null`).
4. Added a **"Water source" dropdown** in the form, right after Soil pH, with common Andhra Pradesh options (Borewell, Open well, Rain-fed, Canal, River, Pond/Tank, Drip irrigation), bilingual EN/TE labels — consistent with the other dropdowns in the app.

I verified no farmer currently has a `water_source` value, so the dropdown can't silently drop pre-existing free-text data, and the moderator side doesn't set this field either — so there's no conflict.

Now: a farmer opens **Edit Profile → Water source**, picks one, saves, and it appears under the **Story** tab on their public profile. This is purely a frontend change (the column already exists), so it works on next deploy with no migration.

Want me to commit this? Note you now have several uncommitted changes stacked up across these tasks (Today's-orders fix, Spices/Other categories, farmer progress tracker, farm-photo RLS, and this) — I can commit them as separate logical commits if you'd like.

```
46	// Shrink large camera photos before upload so they stay quick on a 4G connection.
47	async function compressImage(file: File, maxPx = 800, quality = 0.7): Promise<File> {
48	  return new Promise((resolve) => {
49	    const img = new Image()
50	    const blobUrl = URL.createObjectURL(file)
51	    img.onload = () => {
52	      URL.revokeObjectURL(blobUrl)
53	      const scale = Math.min(1, maxPx / Math.max(img.naturalWidth, img.naturalHeight))
54	      const canvas = document.createElement('canvas')
55	      canvas.width = Math.round(img.naturalWidth * scale)
56	      canvas.height = Math.round(img.naturalHeight * scale)
57	      const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d')
58	      if (!ctx) { resolve(file); return }
59	      ctx.drawImage(img, 0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height)
60	      canvas.toBlob(
61	        (blob) => {
62	          if (!blob) { resolve(file); return }
63	          const name = file.name.replace(/\.[^.]+$/, '.jpg')
64	          resolve(new File([blob], name, { type: 'image/jpeg' }))
65	        },
66	        'image/jpeg',
67	        quality,
68	      )
69	    }
70	    img.onerror = () => { URL.revokeObjectURL(blobUrl); resolve(file) }
71	    img.src = blobUrl
72	  })
73	}
74	
75	// `file` is a freshly picked image (preview is a blob URL we must revoke);
76	// `existingUrl` is a photo already saved on the farmer (edit mode). Display
77	// prefers the new file's preview, falling back to the existing URL.
78	type PhotoState = { file: File | null; preview: string; existingUrl: string | null }
79	const emptyPhoto = (url: string | null = null): PhotoState => ({ file: null, preview: '', existingUrl: url })
80	const photoDisplay = (p: PhotoState) => (p.file ? p.preview : p.existingUrl ?? '')
```

```
158	  let activation_code = makeActivationCode()
159	  for (let i = 0; i < 20; i++) {
160	    const { data: clash } = await supabase.from('farmers').select('id').eq('activation_code', activation_code).maybeSingle()
161	    if (!clash) break
162	    activation_code = makeActivationCode()
163	  }
164	
165	  // The moderator who is onboarding this farmer (from their signed session).
166	  const registered_by_moderator = getModeratorId(req)
167	
168	  const { data: inserted, error } = await supabase
169	    .from('farmers')
170	    .insert({
171	      slug,
172	      name,
173	      phone: phone || null,
174	      village: village || null,
175	      district: district || null,
176	      method,
177	      story_quote: story_quote || null,
178	      farm_size_acres,
179	      farming_since_year,
```

```
18	
19	// Columns the edit form needs to prefill — the same shape the moderator filled
20	// in when onboarding the farmer.
21	const EDIT_COLUMNS =
22	  'id, slug, name, phone, village, district, method, active, story_quote, ' +
23	  'farm_size_acres, farming_since_year, farm_address, soil_organic_carbon, ' +
24	  'upi_id, cod_enabled, bank_account_number, bank_ifsc, pickup_locations, pickup_slots, ' +
25	  'cover_photo_url, photo_url, pesticide_cert_url, upi_qr_code_url, ' +
26	  'lat, lng, location_name'
27	
28	// GET — a single farmer's full record, scoped to the moderator's zone so the
29	// edit form can prefill. Returns 404 for farmers outside the zone.
30	export async function GET(req: NextRequest, { params }: { params: Promise<{ id: string }> }) {
31	  if (!isModeratorRequest(req)) {
32	    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Moderator login required.' }, { status: 401 })
33	  }
34	  const { id } = await params
35	  const zone = getModeratorZone(req)
36	  const supabase = svc()
37	
38	  const { data: farmer, error } = await supabase
39	    .from('farmers')
40	    .select(`${EDIT_COLUMNS}, region_slug`)
41	    .eq('id', id)
42	    .maybeSingle() as { data: ({ region_slug?: string } & Record<string, unknown>) | null; error: { message: string } | null }
43	
44	  if (error) {
45	    console.error('[YFF moderator/farmers GET] failed:', error.message)
46	    return NextResponse.json({ error: error.message }, { status: 500 })
47	  }
48	  if (!farmer || farmer.region_slug !== zone) {
49	    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Farmer not found in your zone.' }, { status: 404 })
50	  }
51	
52	  // Don't leak the internal zone field back to the client.
53	  delete farmer.region_slug
54	  return NextResponse.json({ farmer })
55	}
56	
57	// PATCH — edit farmer details or toggle active status. Scoped to the zone so a
58	// moderator can never touch a farmer outside their own region. Only the fields
59	// present in the body are written, so partial updates (e.g. just `active`) are
60	// safe. Mirrors the validation in the POST (create) handler.
61	export async function PATCH(req: NextRequest, { params }: { params: Promise<{ id: string }> }) {
62	  if (!isModeratorRequest(req)) {
63	    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Moderator login required.' }, { status: 401 })
64	  }
65	  const { id } = await params
66	  const zone = getModeratorZone(req)
67	  const supabase = svc()
68	
69	  // Confirm the farmer belongs to this moderator's zone before any write.
70	  const { data: farmer } = await supabase
71	    .from('farmers')
72	    .select('id, region_slug')
73	    .eq('id', id)
74	    .maybeSingle()
75	  if (!farmer || farmer.region_slug !== zone) {
76	    return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Farmer not found in your zone.' }, { status: 404 })
77	  }
78	
79	  const body = await req.json().catch(() => null)
80	  if (!body) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Invalid body.' }, { status: 400 })
81	  const b = body as Record<string, unknown>
82	
83	  const update: Record<string, unknown> = {}
84	
85	  // Core text fields.
86	  if ('name' in b) {
87	    const name = String(b.name ?? '').trim()
88	    if (!name) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Name cannot be empty.' }, { status: 400 })
89	    update.name = name
90	  }
91	  if ('phone' in b) update.phone = String(b.phone ?? '').trim() || null
92	  if ('village' in b) update.village = String(b.village ?? '').trim() || null
93	  if ('district' in b) update.district = String(b.district ?? '').trim() || null
94	  if ('story_quote' in b) update.story_quote = String(b.story_quote ?? '').trim() || null
95	  if ('farm_address' in b) update.farm_address = String(b.farm_address ?? '').trim() || null
96	  if ('farm_size_acres' in b) update.farm_size_acres = Number(b.farm_size_acres) || null
97	  if ('farming_since_year' in b) update.farming_since_year = Number(b.farming_since_year) || null
98	  if ('soil_organic_carbon' in b) {
99	    update.soil_organic_carbon = Number(b.soil_organic_carbon) > 0 ? Number(b.soil_organic_carbon) : null
100	  }
101	  if ('active' in b) update.active = Boolean(b.active)
102	  if ('cod_enabled' in b) update.cod_enabled = b.cod_enabled === true
103	  if ('method' in b) {
104	    const m = String(b.method ?? '')
105	    if ((METHODS as readonly string[]).includes(m)) update.method = m
106	  }
107	
108	  // Payout details — validated the same way as on create.
109	  if ('upi_id' in b) {
110	    const upi = String(b.upi_id ?? '').trim()
111	    if (upi && !/^[a-zA-Z0-9._-]{2,256}@[a-zA-Z]{2,64}$/.test(upi)) {
112	      return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Invalid UPI ID. Example: name@ybl' }, { status: 400 })
113	    }
114	    update.upi_id = upi || null
115	  }
116	  if ('bank_account_number' in b) {
117	    update.bank_account_number = String(b.bank_account_number ?? '').trim() || null
118	  }
119	  if ('bank_ifsc' in b) {
120	    const ifsc = String(b.bank_ifsc ?? '').trim().toUpperCase()
121	    if (ifsc && !/^[A-Z]{4}0[A-Z0-9]{6}$/.test(ifsc)) {
122	      return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Invalid IFSC. Example: SBIN0001234' }, { status: 400 })
123	    }
124	    update.bank_ifsc = ifsc || null
125	  }
126	
127	  // Pickup locations — dedupe and drop blanks, as on create.
128	  if ('pickup_locations' in b) {
129	    const raw = b.pickup_locations
130	    update.pickup_locations = Array.isArray(raw)
131	      ? Array.from(new Set(raw.map((p) => String(p).trim()).filter(Boolean)))
132	      : []
133	  }
134	
135	  // Pickup schedule — a per-location map { [location]: PickupSlot[] }. Scope to
136	  // the locations sent alongside (falling back to the map's own keys), drop
137	  // empty windows, and store null when empty so the column stays tidy.
138	  if ('pickup_slots' in b) {
139	    const locs = Array.isArray(b.pickup_locations)
140	      ? b.pickup_locations.map((p) => String(p).trim()).filter(Boolean)
141	      : undefined
142	    const clean = normalizePickupSchedule(b.pickup_slots, locs)
143	    update.pickup_slots = Object.keys(clean).length > 0 ? clean : null
144	  }
145	
146	  // Photos — the client sends the resolved URL (existing, newly uploaded, or
147	  // null to clear).
```

> AGENT

The full chain is wired. Done.

## What I added

The moderator's farmer create/edit form was missing two fields that exist on the farmer's own profile editor: **Soil pH** and **Water source**. I added both across the full stack so they save and prefill correctly:

**1. Moderator form (`ModeratorFarmerForm.tsx`)**
- Added `soil_ph` + `water_source` to the `FarmerInitial` type, blank defaults, and form state.
- Added a **Soil pH** number input (next to Soil organic carbon) and a **Water source** dropdown with the same options as the farmer side (Borewell, Open well, Rain-fed, Canal, River, Pond/Tank, Drip irrigation).

**2. Create API (`POST /api/moderator/farmers`)** — parses `soil_ph` (numeric > 0) and `water_source` (trimmed string), inserts both.

**3. Edit API (`PATCH /api/moderator/farmers/[id]`)** — added both to `EDIT_COLUMNS` (so the edit form prefills) and to the partial-update handler.

**4. Edit page (`moderator/farmers/[id]/edit`)** — added both to the `FarmerRow` type and the `toInitial()` mapping.

I verified the data flow end-to-end: GET returns the new columns → form prefills → PATCH/POST persists them. Both DB columns already exist (`soil_ph` numeric, `water_source` varchar), so **no migration is needed** — this works on next deploy.

## On "all the farmer profile data"
I compared the two forms field-by-field. With these two added, the moderator form now covers **every** field the farmer's own editor has (name, village, district, method, farm size, farming-since, soil carbon, soil pH, water source, story, pickup locations/slots, farm address, GPS, UPI/QR, COD, cover/avatar/cert photos) — plus extras only the moderator has (bank account/IFSC). So nothing else is missing; soil pH and water source were the only gaps.

Want me to commit this? You've now got 6 distinct changes stacked uncommitted — I'd suggest committing them as separate logical commits when you're ready.

```
487	    if (!r) return { ok: false, error: 'Network error. Please try again.' }
488	    const json = await r.json().catch(() => ({}))
489	    if (!r.ok || !json?.ok) return { ok: false, error: json?.error ?? 'Could not place order.' }
490	    // Order saved — drop the key so a genuinely new order later gets a fresh one.
491	    delete idempotencyKeys.current[f.farmerId]
492	    return { ok: true, orderIds: json.orderIds, total: json.total }
```

```
69	  if (!body) return bad('Invalid request body.')
70	  const { farmerId, paymentMethod, pickupLocation, items } = body

79	  }

82	  for (const it of items) {
83	    if (!it || typeof it !== 'object') return bad('Invalid item.')
84	    if (!it.listingId || !UUID_RE.test(it.listingId)) return bad('Invalid listing id.')
85	    if (!Number.isFinite(it.qty) || it.qty <= 0 || it.qty > 10000) return bad('Invalid quantity.')
86	  }

88	  // Guest checkout: no session, so the buyer's identity comes from the request.
89	  // We still never trust the client for prices or stock — only for who they are.
90	  const isGuest = !session
91	  let guestName: string | null = null
92	  let guestEmail: string | null = null
93	  let guestPhone: string | null = null
94	  if (isGuest) {
95	    guestName = String(body.guest?.name ?? '').trim().slice(0, 80)
96	    guestEmail = String(body.guest?.email ?? '').trim().toLowerCase().slice(0, 200)
97	    guestPhone = normalizePhone(body.guest?.phone)
98	    if (!guestName) return bad('Please enter your name.')

100	    if (!guestPhone) return bad('Enter a valid 10-digit mobile number.')
```

> AGENT

Type-check passes. Done.

## Summary — City/Town added to the delivery location

**Database:** Added a `delivery_city` text column to `orders` (applied live to Supabase + saved as `scripts/orders-delivery-city-migration.sql`).

**Checkout input (`Cart.tsx`):**
- New **"City / Town"** field in the delivery-address form (between Full address and Landmark), bilingual EN/TE label, placeholder "e.g. Guntur".
- Made it **required for delivery** (home delivery / courier) — same as address and pincode — with client-side validation and an updated inline warning.
- Included `deliveryCity` in the order payload.

**Order placement (`/api/orders/place`):** Parses `deliveryCity` (trimmed, max 100 chars), validates it's present for delivery orders ("Enter your city or town."), and stores it in `delivery_city`. This is the single write path for all payment methods (UPI/COD/Razorpay), so every order captures it.

**Display — surfaced everywhere the delivery address shows** (🏙️ city line, right under the address):
- Farmer order detail (+ its column list)
- Consumer order detail (+ its API select)
- Rider dashboard (+ its API select & types)
- Admin deliveries (+ its API select)

Everything type-checks. The column is live, so this works on next deploy with no further migration.

Two notes:
- The new field is **required for delivery orders**, so buyers can't check out for home delivery without a city. If you'd rather keep it optional (like Landmark), say so and I'll relax the validation.
- Existing orders placed before this will simply have no city (the line is hidden when empty) — no backfill.

Want me to commit this? You now have 7 distinct changes uncommitted; I can split them into logical commits whenever you're ready.

> AGENT

Type-check passes. Done.

## What I added

The public farmer profile (`/farmer/[slug]`, opened via the dashboard's "View profile") had a **"Farm details"** grid in `StoryTab` that was missing the phone. I added a **Phone (WhatsApp)** detail to that grid:

- Shows `+91 {phone}` as a **tap-to-WhatsApp link** (opens the same `wa.me` chat the page's other WhatsApp buttons use), so buyers can both see and message the number.
- Only renders when the farmer has a phone on file (no empty "—" row).
- Bilingual label — "Phone (WhatsApp)" / "ఫోన్ (వాట్సాప్)", reusing the existing `whatsapp` translation.

Implementation detail: I widened the grid's `value` type to `ReactNode` so the phone cell can be a link rather than plain text, and moved the `whatsappVisitLink` definition above the grid so it can be reused.

I confirmed the profile page fetches the farmer with `select('*')`, so `phone` is available. Type-check passes. This is a frontend-only change — works on next deploy, no migration.

Per your selections, I added **only** Phone (WhatsApp) — I left Soil pH / Soil organic carbon / Farm address off the public profile. If you want those shown there too later, it's a one-line addition each.

Want me to commit this? You've now got 8 distinct changes stacked uncommitted across these tasks — happy to split them into logical commits whenever you're ready.

> AGENT

Type-check passes. Done.

## What I changed (`Cart.tsx`)

The Trello card had two parts:

**1. "Highlight the price against each ordered item"** — this was missing. Each cart line previously showed only the unit price (`₹60/kg`). I changed it to show the computed line price, highlighted:

> `₹60/kg × 1 = ` **`₹60`**

The line total (qty × unit price) is bold green so it stands out against each item. It uses `it.pricePerKg`, which already reflects any active bulk/tier price, so the "Bulk price applied" case stays correct and the line prices always sum to the total.

**2. "Show the item total at the end"** — this already existed:
- No-fee orders show **"Estimated total ₹61"** (visible in your screenshot).
- Orders with delivery/platform fees show a breakdown: **"Subtotal"** (the item total) → fees → **"Total"**.

So the item total was already rendered right after the items; the per-item price was the genuine gap.

Frontend-only, type-checks, works on next deploy. Want me to commit this? That's 9 distinct changes stacked now — I can split them into logical commits whenever you're ready.

```
3	import { useRouter } from 'next/navigation'
4	import { useLang } from '@/lib/LanguageContext'

6	
7	export default function TopNav({ regionSlug }: { regionSlug: string }) {
8	  const { tx, L } = useLang()
9	  const router = useRouter()
10	
11	  const handleBack = () => {
12	    if (window.history.length > 1) {
13	      router.back()
14	    } else {
15	      router.push(regionSlug ? `/region/${regionSlug}` : '/consumer')
16	    }
17	  }
18	
19	  return (
20	    <nav className="sticky top-0 z-50 bg-white border-b border-gray-200 px-4 py-3 flex items-center justify-between">
21	      <button
22	        onClick={handleBack}
23	        className="flex items-center gap-2 text-gray-600 active:text-gray-900 min-h-[44px]"
```

I've traced all three. Here's my understanding:

## What I understand

**Bug 1 — Cover photo is cropped, not showing full.**
`FarmCover.tsx:55` renders the cover in a fixed short box (`h-44` ≈ 176px) with `object-cover`. On a wide screen that short-but-very-wide box forces `object-cover` to crop the photo heavily (top & bottom cut off). You want the whole photo visible. This is a design trade-off, so I want to confirm how.

**Bug 2 — EN/తె language toggle barely visible.**
`LanguageToggle.tsx` is styled for a **dark green** header — container `bg-white/10`, inactive text `text-white/80`, active pill `bg-white`. But on the farmer profile, `TopNav` has a **white** background. So white-on-white makes it nearly invisible: the "తె" option disappears entirely and "EN" only shows faintly. The same toggle looks fine on the farmer dashboard (green header), so I'll add a **light variant** for white backgrounds (dark text + visible border) rather than break the dark one. ✓ clear

**Bug 3 — Remove the "Chemicals" stat.**
`TrustStrip.tsx:30` adds a `{ label: tx.chemicals, value: chemicalListings }` tile (the "0 Chemicals" you see). I'll remove that tile and switch the grid from 5 to 4 columns. ✓ clear

Bugs 2 and 3 are unambiguous — I'll fix those. For bug 1, how do you want the cover handled?

All three fixed, type-check passes.

## Summary

**Bug 1 — Cover photo cropped → now taller, fills edge-to-edge** (`FarmCover.tsx`)
Made the cover height responsive: `h-44` on phones (unchanged, stays compact) → `sm:h-60 md:h-72 lg:h-80` on wider screens. Kept `object-cover`, so the photo still fills the full width but the taller box means far less top/bottom gets cropped on desktop. Much more of the farm photo is visible.

**Bug 2 — EN/తె toggle invisible on white → light variant** (`LanguageToggle.tsx` + `TopNav.tsx`)
The toggle was styled for a dark green header (white pill, white text), so on the profile's **white** `TopNav` it was white-on-white. I added a `variant` prop:
- `dark` (default) — unchanged, used by the green dashboard header.
- `light` — grey track, **green active pill with white text**, grey inactive text — clearly visible on white.

`TopNav` now passes `variant="light"`. Both EN and తె are now legible.

**Bug 3 — Removed the "Chemicals" stat** (`TrustStrip.tsx` + `page.tsx`)
Dropped the "0 Chemicals" tile, changed the grid from 5 → 4 columns (Years farming · Star rating · Buyers · Produce now), and removed the now-unused `chemicalListings` prop and its computation from the page.

All changes are frontend-only — no migration, works on next deploy. Want me to commit these (plus the still-uncommitted earlier changes)?

```
=== platform-fee.ts ===
// Platform fee (a.k.a. moderator commission). A global percentage the moderator
// sets, added on top of the consumer's order subtotal and shown as a
// "Platform fee". Lives in the single-row platform_settings table; each order
// stamps the resolved rupee amount in orders.platform_fee.

import type { SupabaseClient } from '@supabase/supabase-js'

// Resolve the rupee fee for a given subtotal. Rounded to whole rupees; never
// negative. pct is a percentage (5 → 5%).
export function computePlatformFee(subtotal: number, pct: number): number {
  if (!pct || pct <= 0 || !Number.isFinite(pct)) return 0
  if (!subtotal || subtotal <= 0) return 0
  return Math.round((subtotal * pct) / 100)
}

// Read the current fee percentage. Best-effort: if the table doesn't exist yet
// (migration not applied) or anything fails, fall back to 0 so checkout and
// payments are never blocked by the fee.
export async function getPlatformFeePercent(supabase: SupabaseClient): Promise<number> {
  try {
    const { data, error } = await supabase
      .from('platform_settings')
      .select('fee_percent')
      .eq('id', 1)
      .maybeSingle()
    if (error) return 0
    const pct = Number(data?.fee_percent)
    return Number.isFinite(pct) && pct > 0 ? pct : 0
  } catch {
    return 0
  }
}
```

> AGENT

The moderator route uses the **service-role key** (bypasses RLS), so I can safely lock the table: enable RLS with public read only. That keeps the Cart's read and the moderator's write working, but stops anon clients from tampering with the fee. Let me apply that.

> TOOL

tool_use mcp__claude_ai_Supabase__apply_migration
id: toolu_01WU9bJbbdzoArimG9C86SZN
```json
{
  "project_id": "bzwczufnlqwlirtrccwr",
  "name": "platform_settings_rls_read_only",
  "query": "-- platform_settings holds a money setting (the platform fee %). Without RLS,\n-- any holder of the anon key could write it via PostgREST. Enable RLS with\n-- public READ only; the moderator API writes it with the service-role key,\n-- which bypasses RLS, so writes keep working while anon can only read.\nalter table public.platform_settings enable row level security;\n\ndrop policy if exists \"Public read platform settings\" on public.platform_settings;\ncreate policy \"Public read platform settings\" on public.platform_settings\n  for select using (true);"
}
```

Done. The platform-fee migration is applied and verified.

## What I ran
1. **Created `platform_settings`** (single-row config table) and **seeded** it with `id=1, fee_percent=0` (so prod defaults to *no* fee until you set one).
2. **Added `orders.platform_fee`** column (per-order stamp, default 0).
3. **Secured it with RLS** — enabled row-level security with a **public read-only** policy. The moderator API writes the fee with the service-role key (which bypasses RLS), so the moderator can still update it, but no one with the anon key can tamper with the money setting.

## Verification
- `seeded_fee_percent`: **0** ✓ (safe default)
- `rls_enabled`: **true** ✓
- policy: **Public read platform settings (select)** ✓ — Cart can read it, anon can't write it
- `orders.platform_fee` column exists ✓

The platform-fee feature now has its full schema in prod, matching the deployed code. The fee shows as 0% everywhere until a moderator sets a percentage via the moderator panel — at which point it'll appear as a "Platform fee" line in checkout and stamp onto each order.

All migrations from this session are now applied (media RLS, delivery_city, platform fee). Nothing else pending.

```
55	export default async function FarmerPage({ params }: { params: Promise<{ slug: string }> }) {
56	  const { slug } = await params
57	
58	  const { data: farmer } = await supabase
59	    .from('farmers')
60	    .select('*')
61	    .eq('slug', slug)
62	    .eq('active', true)
63	    .single()
64	
65	  if (!farmer) notFound()
66	
67	  const { data: produce } = await supabase
68	    .from('produce_listings')
69	    .select('*')
70	    .eq('farmer_id', farmer.id)
71	    .order('created_at', { ascending: false })
72	
73	  const { data: reviews } = await supabase
74	    .from('reviews')
75	    .select('*')
76	    .eq('farmer_id', farmer.id)
77	    .eq('approved', true)
78	    .order('created_at', { ascending: false })
79	
80	  // Verified per-order buyer feedback (the ⭐ button consumers tap on their
81	  // orders) lives in produce_reviews, tied to a listing. Only feedback on a
82	  // completed order is approved/public. Surface it on the farmer's profile too —
83	  // tagged with the produce it was about — so consumer feedback isn't invisible
84	  // here, and fold it into the same Reviews tab + star average as written reviews.
85	  const { data: produceReviews } = await supabase
86	    .from('produce_reviews')
87	    .select('id, reviewer_name, star_rating, review_text, created_at, produce_listing_id')
88	    .eq('farmer_id', farmer.id)
89	    .eq('approved', true)
90	    .order('created_at', { ascending: false })
91	
92	  // Resolve the produce names with a separate query rather than a PostgREST
93	  // embedded join (produce_reviews → produce_listings). That relationship isn't
94	  // in the schema cache, so embedding it made the whole produce_reviews query
95	  // error out and return null — which silently hid ALL verified buyer feedback
96	  // from the profile (showing "No reviews yet" and a 0 rating even when approved
97	  // reviews existed).
98	  const listingIds = [
99	    ...new Set((produceReviews ?? []).map((r) => r.produce_listing_id).filter(Boolean) as string[]),
100	  ]
101	  const { data: listingNames } = listingIds.length
102	    ? await supabase.from('produce_listings').select('id, name').in('id', listingIds)
103	    : { data: [] as { id: string; name: string }[] }
104	  const nameById = new Map((listingNames ?? []).map((l) => [l.id, l.name]))
105	
106	  const mappedProduceReviews = (produceReviews ?? []).map((r) => ({
107	    id: r.id,
108	    reviewer_name: r.reviewer_name,
109	    star_rating: r.star_rating,
110	    review_text: r.review_text,
111	    produce_ordered: r.produce_listing_id ? nameById.get(r.produce_listing_id) ?? null : null,
112	    created_at: r.created_at,
113	  }))
114	
115	  // Both sources, newest first — one combined list for the Reviews tab.
116	  const allReviews = [...(reviews ?? []), ...mappedProduceReviews].sort(
117	    (a, b) => new Date(b.created_at as string).getTime() - new Date(a.created_at as string).getTime(),
118	  )
119	
120	  // Per-listing rating summary so the Produce tab can show each produce's own
121	  // verified buyer feedback (avg stars, count, latest snippet) on its card —
122	  // produceReviews is already ordered newest-first, so the first hit per listing
123	  // is the latest review.
124	  const reviewAcc = new Map<string, { sum: number; count: number; latest: { name: string; text: string | null; stars: number } | null }>()
125	  for (const r of produceReviews ?? []) {
126	    if (!r.produce_listing_id) continue
127	    const cur = reviewAcc.get(r.produce_listing_id) ?? { sum: 0, count: 0, latest: null }
128	    cur.sum += (r.star_rating as number) ?? 0
129	    cur.count += 1
130	    if (!cur.latest) cur.latest = { name: r.reviewer_name, text: r.review_text, stars: (r.star_rating as number) ?? 0 }
131	    reviewAcc.set(r.produce_listing_id, cur)
132	  }
133	  const produceReviewSummary: Record<string, { avg: number; count: number; latest: { name: string; text: string | null; stars: number } | null }> = {}
134	  reviewAcc.forEach((v, k) => {
135	    produceReviewSummary[k] = { avg: Math.round((v.sum / v.count) * 10) / 10, count: v.count, latest: v.latest }
136	  })
137	
138	  // Star rating shown in the trust strip reflects all real feedback (written
139	  // reviews + verified order feedback). Falls back to the stored value if there
140	  // are no reviews yet.
141	  const ratingAvg =
142	    allReviews.length > 0
143	      ? Math.round(
144	          (allReviews.reduce((sum, r) => sum + ((r.star_rating as number) ?? 0), 0) / allReviews.length) * 10,
145	        ) / 10
146	      : (farmer.rating_avg ?? 0)
147	
148	  const { data: media } = await supabase
149	    .from('media')
150	    .select('*')
151	    .eq('farmer_id', farmer.id)
152	    .order('sort_order', { ascending: true })
153	
154	  return (
155	    <main className="min-h-screen bg-gray-50 pb-24">
156	      <TopNav regionSlug={farmer.region_slug} />
157	      <FarmCover farmer={farmer} />
158	      {/* Count only live listings — a produce the farmer has suspended (or that
159	          is still "coming soon") must not inflate the public "Produce now" stat. */}
160	      <TrustStrip
161	        farmer={{ ...farmer, rating_avg: ratingAvg }}
162	        produceCount={(produce ?? []).filter((p) => (p as { status?: string }).status === 'available').length}
163	      />
164	      <Suspense fallback={null}>
```

```json
  "project_id": "bzwczufnlqwlirtrccwr",
  "name": "farmer_buyer_count_function",
  "query": "-- The farmer profile \"Buyers\" stat read farmers.buyer_count, a denormalized\n-- column that was never maintained (always 0). Orders are RLS-locked, so the\n-- public profile's anon client can't count them directly. Expose a SECURITY\n-- DEFINER function that returns only the distinct-buyer count (registered\n-- consumers by id + guests by phone) for a farmer — no PII leaves the DB.\ncreate or replace function public.farmer_buyer_count(p_farmer_id uuid)\nreturns integer\nlanguage sql\nsecurity definer\nset search_path = public\nstable\nas $$\n  select count(distinct coalesce(consumer_id::text, buyer_phone))::int\n  from public.orders\n  where farmer_id = p_farmer_id;\n$$;\n\ngrant execute on function public.farmer_buyer_count(uuid) to anon, authenticated;"
}
```

> AGENT

Done. The "Buyers" stat now shows the real count.

## Root cause
The profile's "Buyers" tile read `farmers.buyer_count` — a denormalized column that was **never maintained**, so it was `0` for every farmer. Meanwhile veeru actually has **5** distinct buyers and kapil **4**.

## Fix
Mirrors how the page already computes the live star-rating (overriding its stale stored column):

1. **DB function** (applied live + saved as `scripts/farmer-buyer-count-function.sql`): `farmer_buyer_count(farmer_id)` — a `SECURITY DEFINER` SQL function that counts **distinct consumers who ordered from that farmer** (registered consumers by `consumer_id` + guests by `buyer_phone`). Orders are RLS-locked, so the public profile's anon client can't read them — this function returns *only an integer count*, no order rows or PII, and is granted to `anon`. Verified it returns 5 and 4.

2. **Profile page** (`farmer/[slug]/page.tsx`): calls the function via `supabase.rpc(...)` and passes the real count into `TrustStrip` as `buyer_count`, overriding the stale `0` (with a fallback to the stored value if the RPC ever fails).

So veeru's profile will now show **5 Buyers** instead of 0. Type-checks; the function is already live, so it works as soon as the frontend deploys.

One definition note: I counted **every** consumer who placed an order (any status), matching the card's "all the Consumers ordering." If you'd rather exclude declined/cancelled orders (i.e., only count buyers who actually completed a purchase), it's a one-line `where` change — let me know.

This is a new uncommitted change. Want me to commit and push it to main with the others?

Renamed the field label in the farmer's Edit Profile form. It still saves to the same `story_quote` column (which the public profile's **About Farm** tab displays), so no migration needed and existing values are preserved — only the label changed.

**2. Removed "How we grow (growing notes)" from the produce form** (Quality parameters section)
Deleted that textarea field. The Quality parameters section now ends at **Chemicals / pesticide**.

I deliberately **kept** the `howWeGrow` state and its place in the save payload (`how_we_grow: howWeGrow.trim() || null`). On editing an existing produce, this preserves whatever `how_we_grow` value was already saved rather than wiping it — the field just isn't shown/editable anymore. New produce simply saves it as null.

If you'd instead prefer to fully drop `how_we_grow` (stop writing it entirely, and let existing values clear on next edit), I can remove the state and payload lines too — but the current approach is non-destructive.

This is a frontend-only change, type-checks clean. Want me to commit this along with the Buyers-stat fix and push to main?

## What I changed (`ProduceTab.tsx`)

When the logged-in farmer views their **own** public profile (e.g. via the dashboard's "View profile" link), the consumer **"Add to cart"** button is now hidden — they can't buy from themselves.

- Added an `isOwner` check: client-side, `localStorage.yff_farmer_id === farmer.id`.
- `onAddToCart` is passed as `undefined` for the owner, and `ProduceCard` now only renders the "Add to cart" button when `onAddToCart` is provided (made the prop optional and guarded `handleAdd`).

Behavior:
- **Consumer / buyer** viewing the profile → Add to cart shows ✓
- **Farmer viewing their own profile** → Add to cart hidden ✓
- **Farmer viewing another farmer's profile** → Add to cart still shows (a farmer can buy from others; the card was specifically about the farmer's own produce)

`isOwner` starts `false` so server and initial client render match (no hydration mismatch); the effect flips it after mount for the owner. Frontend-only, type-checks clean.

There are now several uncommitted changes stacked (Buyers-stat fix + function, Story→How we grow / produce field removal, and this). Want me to commit them and push to main?

## What I changed (`Cart.tsx` — Payment Successful screen)

**Removed:** the "Paid to" label and the farmer's name + village (per "no need of name of farmer").

**Added:** a **Transaction ID** on the success screen, sourced correctly per payment type:
- **Online (Razorpay):** the verified `razorpay_payment_id`
- **Manual UPI:** the buyer-entered **UTR**
- **COD:** no transaction id (none exists), so the line is hidden

The success box now reads:
```
Amount paid
₹61
Transaction ID
pay_Qk7f2... (monospace, wraps)
```

How it's wired:
1. Added an optional `transactionId` to the payment state.
2. The Razorpay handler stamps it with the payment id once the signature is verified server-side.
3. The manual-UPI confirm path stamps it with the UTR the buyer entered.
4. The success screen shows the **Amount** (kept), then the **Transaction ID** when present — bilingual labels (EN/TE).

For the COD/"Order placed" variant, the farmer name is also gone (consistent with your instruction) and it shows "Amount to pay ₹X" with no transaction id.

Frontend-only, type-checks clean. There are now quite a few uncommitted changes piled up (Buyers fix + DB function, How-we-grow, Add-to-cart hide, Resolved filter, this). Want me to commit them all and push to main?

## Shipped (5 commits)
1. **Buyers count fix** — profile now shows the real distinct-buyer count via the `farmer_buyer_count` DB function (already applied live to Supabase, so it's in sync with this deploy).
2. **"Picked up" filter → "Resolved"** on the farmer orders page.
3. **Farmer dashboard** — Story→"How we grow" rename, removed the produce "growing notes" field, and **removed the inline "Your produce" section** (per your pick). Cleaned up the now-dead component, helper, and import. Produce management still lives on the **Active listings** tile → Manage modal.
4. **Hide "Add to cart"** when a farmer views their own profile.
5. **Payment Successful page** — removed "Paid to" + farmer name, added **Transaction ID** (Razorpay payment id online, or the UTR for manual UPI).

Production build passed before pushing; all changes type-check.

## Note on what you'll see live
These were the changes that were sitting committed-but-not-deployed — including the **"Resolved" filter** you re-flagged. Once Vercel finishes deploying commit `cfe59fe` (and your browser/PWA picks up the new version — a hard refresh helps), all of these go live together.

No pending migrations — the only DB change this round (`farmer_buyer_count`) is already applied. Everything's clean.
